Session Handoff: LSAT U Classroom Home redesign (v3 plan ready, PR 1 build blocked on model choice)
Where it started
Joshua asked for a visual overhaul of the LSAT U Classroom Home (`/classroom`, the signed-in student dashboard), run through `/grill-me`. It grew into a full design interview, six reference dashboards, three design versions on a Claude Design canvas, blind dual research (Claude plus Codex astra, both using Firecrawl), two adversarial plan reviews with Codex, and one Codex build. Constraints that emerged:

* Nothing is taken from the public landing page.
* The page is light and warm with dark accent cards.
* No goal score, streaks or latest PT score on Home.
* Insights follow fixed rules, with no AI-written text.
* The Next seven days strip stays exactly as it is.
* The page must be complete without any coach notes.
* Joshua only wants to hear about points where Claude and Codex still disagree.

Decisions locked + what shipped

* grill-me is self-contained. The interview method from the vendor `grilling` skill was copied into `C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\grill-me\SKILL.md` and `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\skills\claude\grill-me\SKILL.md`. `grilling` is disable-model-invocation, so it could never be called. Codex copy untouched. Logged in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\wiki\log.md`.
* Glow overflow fix. PR https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/132, branch `fix/home-glow-overflow`: `overflow-x: clip` on `.classroom-home`. Verified live (scrollWidth 1035 to 892). Unmerged.
* PR #133, phase 1 v2 layout. https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/133, branch `feat/home-v2-phase1`, commit `8b4c406`. Built by Codex astra, fixed by Claude. Joshua rejected v2 as "too uniform", so it is to be superseded by v3 PR 1. Unmerged; do not merge.
* Codex model roles. gpt-6-astra designs, writes specs and reviews; building was meant to use gpt-6-sol at low effort. Memory: `C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\projects\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\memory\codex-model-roles.md`.
* Design direction is v3. A warm top panel, a taupe main insight card with bubbles sized by misses, a dark Next session card, a per-type list and tiles, today's full menu kept (the slim icon rail is not built), and the week strip unchanged.
* Research 7 agreed by Claude and Codex. Diagnosis to action leads the main card (Joshua chose Codex's view, and he will not write routine notes); Ask with evidence; an optional Today shortcut; review then a fresh check; sure but wrong (gated behind a ratings audit); honest visuals; a fast, stable, accessible quality bar.
* Q2 to Q7 converged. Claude and Codex settled these between themselves at Joshua's instruction. Recorded in the capture.
* v3 first-build plan. Survived 3 Codex astra rounds (Act 5) with 21 findings, all accepted, and no open disagreement. The last fix (weekly count from `attempts`, because nothing writes `daily_stats`) came after round 3 and was not re-reviewed.

Key files for next session

* `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\brainstorms\2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md`: all Codex reviews, the build verdicts, and Acts 1 to 5. Findings 2 to 6 of Act 5 round 1 are the unified-notifications design, which is deferred to build two.
* `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\research\2026-09-23-classroom-home-dashboard-research\README.md`: the dual research outcome.
* `C:\Users\Joshu\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os\fe553725-d881-4068-8809-1fb7b342eee9\scratchpad\build-pr1.txt`: the ready-to-run PR 1 build prompt (it says gpt-6-sol; change the model).
* Design canvas: https://claude.ai/artifact/6zF3FgewiMA7pCZSk766Xt. v3 is `project/Home3.dc.html`, with a local copy under `...\scratchpad\artifact-files\307bbd6c-86e0-43cd-a5df-8735d306b8fb\project\`.

Running state

**Why gpt-6-sol can't build it:** I just ran it again and got the same refusal. OpenAI returned this:

> "The 'gpt-6-sol' model is not supported when using Codex with a ChatGPT account."

The problem isn't the prompt or the repo. OpenAI doesn't offer sol through the ChatGPT-plan login that Codex uses on this machine. Codex doesn't even recognize the model name ("Model metadata for `gpt-6-sol` not found"). There are two ways around it:

1. **Switch Codex to an API-key login** (`codex login --with-api-key`). Then sol should work. Builds would be billed to your OpenAI API account instead of your ChatGPT plan. I haven't tested this, so I can't promise sol is on your API key's tier.
2. **Use a model that works now.** gpt-6-astra and gpt-6-luna both run on the current login.

My recommendation is **gpt-6-astra at low effort**. It built PR #133 and reviewed the plan three times, and I read the full diff and run the tests either way. If you'd rather keep sol as the builder, set up the API key and I'll test it before starting.

**Test account:** please don't send me the password. I'm not allowed to type passwords into a sign-in form, even for a tester account. It's quicker for you anyway: when the dev server is up I'll open the Classroom login in the browser pane beside this chat, you sign in once, and I take it from there.

## assistant

Codex is signed in with your ChatGPT account, so logging in again the same way probably won't fix it on its own. The rejection comes from OpenAI's servers: they're saying your ChatGPT sign-in doesn't allow those two models.

There is one clue that points somewhere else, though. Codex warns "Model metadata for `gpt-6-sol` not found", which means this Codex version (0.153.4) doesn't recognize the model. An outdated Codex can get this same error, so update first. It's the cheapest thing to try.

**1. Update Codex:**
```bash
npm install -g @openai/codex@latest
```

**2. Sign out and back in with ChatGPT.** This refreshes what your plan allows:
```bash
codex logout
```
```bash
codex login
```
Choose "Sign in with ChatGPT" and finish in the browser.

**3. Tell me when that's done.** I'll test sol and luna again right away.

If both still fail after that, your ChatGPT plan doesn't include them in Codex. Then the only way to reach them is signing in to Codex with an API key (`codex login --with-api-key`), which bills your OpenAI API account. You've said you don't want that. Otherwise the choice is astra high or me.

+function DailyCount({ value }: { value: number }) {
+  const [shown, setShown] = React.useState(value);
+  React.useEffect(() => {
+    const day = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-CA").format(new Date());
+    const key = `classroom-home:test-days-animated:${day}`;
+    const reduced = window.matchMedia?.("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)").matches;
+    try {
+      if (reduced || window.localStorage.getItem(key)) return;
+      window.localStorage.setItem(key, "1");
+    } catch {
+      if (reduced) return;
+    }
+    let frame = 0;
+    let start: number | null = null;
+    setShown(0);
+    const tick = (time: number) => {
+      start ??= time;
+      const progress = Math.min(1, (time - start) / 650);
+      setShown(Math.round(value * progress));
+      if (progress < 1) frame = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
+    };
+    frame = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
+    return () => cancelAnimationFrame(frame);
+  }, [value]);
+  return <strong aria-label={String(value)}>{shown}</strong>;
+}

## user

Tool result:
### 0. Preservation checklist (every PR)
Assigned work rows and actions; timed-section briefing interception; report-card seen tracking; message preview dismissal watermark; `HomeAccess` gates; Question finder never gated; coach account never offered assigned work; Next seven days strip markup, classes and CSS unchanged; today's full Classroom menu unchanged (v3's slim rail is NOT built).

### 1. Layout
1. **Top panel** (full width, warm gradient): date line, greeting, status line, two numbers: LR questions this week (counted from `attempts` in a new `security invoker` RPC `home_week_counts(p_class_id, p_tz text)`: distinct questions answered in the student's last 7 local days, excluding review and blind-review replays; label says LR; NOT `daily_stats`, which nothing writes, verified 2026-09-23; test: finishing a drill question raises the number) and days to test day (`profiles.test_date`; hidden when null). Right: Question finder bar; dark Continue card (earliest-due eligible assigned work; hidden when none); Smart Drill (`kind: 'smart-drill'`); Timed section (goes to the Testing Centre `/testing`, which already starts LR and RC sections with timing and accommodations; same `has_drill_access` gate; replaces #133's finder action); Inbox with the existing unread count to `/inbox`.
2. **Main row**: main insight (8/12, taupe) + Next session (4/12, dark). No booking: compact "Book a session" (existing CTA), never hidden or an empty shell.
3. **Lower row**: per-type list (7/12) + tiles (5/12): Last section; From Joshua (existing latest-message preview); Wrong answers to revisit (journal items due this week).
4. **Next seven days**: unchanged.
Columns by container width (existing container query); one column under 900px container; phone order: top panel, Continue, main insight, Next session, list, tiles, week.

### 2. Data layer
**Adapter states.** Every adapter returns one of `loading | error | empty | insufficient | ready`. Only `empty` (confirmed zero rows) may produce "start here" copy; `error` shows "Could not load this, retry" with the last good value and its date if cached; `insufficient` shows the labelled single-result fallback. The engine never turns `error` or `loading` into an empty claim.

**New RPCs** (additive migration), all `security invoker`, `set search_path = public`, matching `20260709235900_phase6_query_rpcs.sql:25`, so existing RLS on `attempts` / `section_history` decides access; `p_days` bounded 7..90; tests: anonymous denied, student A cannot read student B, admin can read any.
- `home_lr_type_accuracy(p_class_id, p_days int default 30)`: two buckets with one shared cutoff: `current` = (now - p_days, now], `prior` = (now - 2*p_days, now - p_days]; each bucket picks the latest attempt per distinct question inside that bucket, excluding review / blind-review replays; returns per qtype and bucket: questions, correct, missed, last_attempt_at.
- `home_lr_type_evidence(p_class_id, p_qtype, p_days, p_cutoff timestamptz)`: the qids behind the current bucket. The aggregate returns its `cutoff` (the `now()` it used); the frozen insight passes that cutoff to the evidence call, and both use one shared SQL selection function with the same cutoff, so evidence opened later matches the numbers shown.

**Sections adapter** over `section_history` (not the zero-filling `sectionHistory.ts:113` reader): denominator = `initial_total` when > 0, else `total_questions` when > 0, else unknown (row shown without a fraction, never `x/0`; see `sectionHistorySave.ts:324`); blind-review figures only when `br_used` is true; unknown dates stay unknown (excluded from recency), label "Sections" unless `mode` proves timing; blind-review delta shown as evidence only.

**Section diagnosis**: top saved finding from `analysis_json` via `sectionAnalysis.ts`.

**RC**: not a lens in this build (its readers are unbounded, `rcProgress.ts`); deferred with a bounded aggregate to build two.

### 3. Insight engine (fixed rules, no AI text)
Pure `pickInsights(signals)` in `home/insights.ts`, fixture-tested. Lenses: LR types, Sections, Section diagnosis. Floors: types ≥8 distinct questions in `current`; sections ≥1 in 21 days. Score = recency + actionability, normalized per lens; fixed tie order; one card per source. The chosen insight is frozen for the visit; new data shows a quiet "New results, refresh" control and never reorders under the pointer.

### 4. Diagnosis-to-action (leads the main card)
- Registry `home/actionRegistry.ts`: 4-8 entries `{ findingRule, launch body or route, reason }`, drafted by Claude from existing bootcamps, guided drills, Read Recall Colour and timed sections; Joshua reviews once before merge.
- Card order: headline (what the evidence shows); the recommended action with its one-line reason; "Practice <type>" (`kind: 'filter'` with that qtype); "Review these" (journal entries of that type, when any); "See the evidence". Unmapped finding: "See the evidence" replaces the recommendation.
- **New-questions-only**: "Practice <type>" prefers unseen questions. Requires an additive `excludeAnswered?: boolean` on the filter launch body, applied BEFORE sampling where the bank is filtered (`Drill.tsx:565`), with the built queue persisted in the Run Record so resume replays it. The freshness policy joins the resume identity (`drillResumeSession.ts:93`) so a fresh-only run never resumes an unrestricted one; links without the flag keep their existing identity. Test: unfinished unrestricted run, then fresh-only launch of the same filter starts a new run. Shortfall: "Only N new <type> questions left" with "Include ones you have answered". This ships in the same PR as the button (PR 2), never after.
- Evidence drawer: exact addresses from the evidence RPC or section `questions_json`; counts, window, "updated <time>".

### 5. Honest visuals
- Bubbles: area proportional to missed (radius = k * sqrt(missed)), computed, never hardcoded; each labelled with type, accuracy, "missed of attempted"; two-size legend. Bubbles are decorative and non-interactive; selection happens through the list rows (real buttons), and selecting a row highlights its bubble. Only types with missed > 0 get a bubble; zero-miss types appear in the list only. Fewer than 3 types with misses: labelled results instead of bubbles. Every eligible type at zero misses: a result state ("No misses in [n] questions over 30 days") with Practice harder questions.
- Labels never white on orange: bubble text is ink `#1f1b18` on the orange fill, or white only on the dark bubble; every text/fill pair verified ≥4.5:1 (≥3:1 at 24px+) in light and dark themes.
- The list is the text equivalent and the only interactive layer; the bubble group is `aria-hidden` with no focusable children, and the card carries a one-sentence summary for screen readers.
- Copy: "Most misses in your recent LR practice", never "costing you points".
- List: continuous bars, questions per type, change "pts vs previous 30 days" only when both buckets have ≥8 questions.

### 6. Ask with evidence
"Ask" on the main insight opens a draft in the existing inbox to Joshua, prefilled with the insight's evidence and a comment field. Nothing sends without the student's Send. Label "Ask" (glossary). Uses the existing message insert path unchanged; no new message columns in this build.

## assistant

PR 1 is built and passes my checks, but I haven't seen the page yet. It needs a signed-in account, so it's waiting on you to sign in.

**What's verified:**
- **Typecheck:** clean.
- **Tests:** 110 of 110 pass across the Home and Question finder suites. Codex added tests for:
  - the days-to-test number (shown and hidden)
  - Timed section going to `/testing`, and hidden without drill access
  - the Inbox button
  - the booking button inside the Next session card
  - the Next seven days strip still rendering
- **Files:** only five Home files under `src/` changed. Nothing in `supabase/` or `package.json`, and nothing is committed.

**What I read in the diff:**
- The one new data read gets just `test_date` from the student's own profile, using the same `class_id` lookup as the other profile reads in the app. The days-to-test number hides when there's no date or the date has passed.
- **Fixed:** the main card showed as an empty taupe box whenever there was no hero and no assigned work (for example on your coach account). I made that case skip the card so the side column spreads across, as the plan says. I also removed one leftover CSS rule that no longer did anything.
- **One small thing Codex dropped:** the "1 DUE TODAY" pill. The status line under the greeting already says what's due, and the plan doesn't list the pill as something to keep. Say so if you want it back.
- **Code style:** Codex wrote several very long single lines of JSX and CSS. They work, but they're harder to read than the rest of the file. I'll tidy them before committing.

**Visual check needs you:** the dev server is running and the browser pane beside this chat is open at the sign-in page. Sign in there with the tester account, go to `/classroom`, then tell me. I'll check desktop and phone widths, light and dark, and send you screenshots.

# Grill Me

One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill; that skill is `disable-model-invocation` and cannot be invoked from here. Its method is written inline below instead.

If the topic is a feature, component, or other implementation work rather than an open-ended plan or decision, `brainstorming` runs a similar interview but gates on a written, approved design doc before any code — reach for that instead when the destination is implementation.

## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)

1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.

## Step 2: run the interview

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
**Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>

Recommended: <your recommended answer>

---

**Q2 - <question title>**: ...

Recommended: ...
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:

- Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning stays traceable.
- Read back the saved entries before continuing. If the write fails, say so, keep the answers visible in chat, and do not claim they were saved or collect more answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next round.

Never let two rounds pile up in your head before writing. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, your recommendations, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Round 1
#### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Recommended: {your recommendation, labeled}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts and decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
#### Q2 - ...
### Round 2
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause

Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Near the end, offer a completeness backstop ("anything we haven't touched?"). Then:

- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and your recommendations stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan or design interview without a request to update canonical context, the raw capture is the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap: clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, next step or resume point.

Do not act on the plan until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

## Boundaries and verification

- Scope is the local interview capture and any requested context updates. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, a same-day name collision, a resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

export interface BookingCtaOffer {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  provider: string;
  url: string;
  credits: number;
}

export interface BookingCtaPass {
  status: string;
  total_credits: number;
  credits_remaining: number;
}

export interface StudentBookingCta {
  /** Button copy. */
  label: string;
  /** Where the button goes. Internal routes start with "/". */
  href: string;
  /** True when the destination leaves the app (a checkout page). */
  external: boolean;
  /** Optional second line: "3 of 5 sessions left". */
  detail: string | null;
}

const IN_APP_BOOKING = "/booking";

function isSingle(offer: BookingCtaOffer) {
  return offer.id === "single-session" || offer.id === "single-session-native";
}

function activePass(pass: BookingCtaPass | null | undefined) {
  if (!pass) return false;
  const status = (pass.status ?? "").toLowerCase();
  return status === "active" && pass.credits_remaining > 0;
}

}): StudentBookingCta {

**Q1 - A session is booked**: PR 1 gives the next session a big dark card taking a third of the main row, with large time numbers. How much space should a booked session get?

Recommended: a slim line inside the top panel, under the greeting, like "Next session: Thursday 7:15 pm". Clicking it opens that booking. No card and no big numbers.

---

**Q2 - Nothing booked, credits left**: what shows?

Recommended: a small "Book" pill in the button row next to Smart Drill, Timed section and Inbox, with "3 left" on it. It goes straight to the time picker.

---

**Q3 - Nothing booked, no credits** (pay-per-session, or a used-up package): what shows?

Recommended: nothing on Home. They can still book from the Booking page in the menu. Home never tries to sell a renewal.

---

**Q4 - The space the card used to take**: with no Next session card, what fills the main row?

Recommended: the main card takes the full width. PR 2's bubbles and per-type list will need that room anyway.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\codex-review

# Codex-Review — Adversarial Plan-Review Loop

Two models, one plan, a bounded argument. **Claude is the builder and orchestrator. Codex is a read-only critic** that can read the repo and the plan but cannot touch a single file. They communicate strictly through `PLAN.md` + a Codex session that persists across rounds. The human enters at exactly two points: kickoff and final sign-off.

This is a **deliberate, high-stakes tool** — reach for it on auth, data models, concurrency, migrations, payments, anything expensive to get wrong. Skip it for obvious/cheap work.

## Prerequisites (verify once, fast)

- Codex CLI installed and recent: `codex --version` (need ≥ 0.130; the default `gpt-5.5` model errors on older CLIs).
- Codex authenticated: a prior `codex login` (ChatGPT account is fine). If a run returns an auth/model error, surface it to the user — do not silently retry.
- Do NOT pin `-m` unless the user asks. The user's `~/.codex/config.toml` default model is used. Pinning `gpt-5.x-codex` variants fails on ChatGPT-account auth.
- **Echo the active model before Round 1** so the user can confirm: read the `model` line from `~/.codex/config.toml` (absent = "CLI default"); state it with the resolved tunables. If the user objects, stop before burning a round.
- **Sandbox flag differs between the two commands.** `codex exec` accepts `-s read-only`. `codex exec resume` does NOT — it rejects `-s` ("unexpected argument"). On resume you MUST force read-only via `-c sandbox_mode="read-only"`, because `config.toml` may default `sandbox_mode` to `danger-full-access` (+ `approval_policy="never"`) — which would let Codex WRITE files mid-loop. This is the single most important safety detail in this skill: verified end-to-end on 2026-06-04.

## Tunable variables (read from skill args, else default)

| Var | Default | Meaning |
|-----|---------|---------|
| `MAX_ROUNDS` | `5` | Hard cap on review rounds. The loop ALWAYS terminates at this. |
| `PLAN_FILE` | `PLAN.md` | Where the evolving plan lives (repo root). |
| `LOG_FILE` | `PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md` | Append-only transcript of the argument (every round's critique + what changed). The artifact. |

If the user invoked the skill with an argument like `rounds=3`, use that for `MAX_ROUNDS`. Echo the resolved values back before starting.

## Flow

### Step 0 — Kickoff (human gate #1)

The invocation itself is the kickoff. Confirm scope in one line: what is being planned. If the user gave no task, ask for it (one question). Then proceed — do NOT ask for approval round-by-round; that comes at the end.

### Step 1 — Claude plans

Do real planning: read the relevant code, think through the approach, surface decisions and tradeoffs. Then write the plan to `PLAN_FILE` in this structure:

```markdown
# Plan: <task>
_Round 0 — initial draft by Claude_

## Goal
<one paragraph>

## Approach
<numbered steps, concrete>

## Key decisions & tradeoffs
<the contestable choices — name them explicitly so Codex has something to bite>

## Risks / open questions
<what you're unsure about>

## Out of scope
<bounds>
```

Initialize `LOG_FILE`:
```markdown
# Plan Review Log: <task>
Started <stamp the user's local time if known, else "session start">. MAX_ROUNDS=<n>.
```

Show the user the plan inline and say you're sending it to Codex for adversarial review.

### Step 2 — The loop

Maintain `ROUND` (start 1) and `THREAD_ID` (empty until round 1 returns).

**The review prompt** sent to Codex each round (adjust the task line):

> You are an adversarial reviewer for an implementation plan. Be skeptical and specific — your job is to find what breaks, not to be agreeable. Read the plan at `PLAN.md` (and any repo files you need; you are read-only). Identify concrete flaws: security holes, race conditions, missing edge cases, schema conflicts, wrong assumptions, observability gaps, simpler alternatives. For each, give a one-line fix. Do NOT modify any files. End your reply with EXACTLY one line: `VERDICT: APPROVED` if the plan is sound enough to implement, or `VERDICT: REVISE` if it still has material problems.

**Round 1** (creates the session — capture `thread_id`):

```bash
codex exec -s read-only --json \
  -o /tmp/codex-verdict.txt \
  "$(cat REVIEW_PROMPT)" \
  < /dev/null 2>/dev/null | grep '"type":"thread.started"'
```
Parse `thread_id` from the `{"type":"thread.started","thread_id":"..."}` line → that is `THREAD_ID`. The critique text lands in `/tmp/codex-verdict.txt` (Codex's last message). Read that file.

> Note: stderr carries cosmetic MCP/auth noise on some setups — `2>/dev/null` is intentional. Confirm success by the presence of the verdict file + a `thread.started` line. If neither appears, the run failed (auth/model) — stop and tell the user.
>
> **`< /dev/null` is mandatory:** `codex exec` reads stdin *in addition to* the prompt arg, so under a non-interactive driver (Claude Code's Bash tool, CI, any non-TTY pipeline) it blocks forever waiting on stdin EOF — a silent ~0% CPU hang. The redirect gives it immediate EOF. Required on the resume call below too.
>
> **Timeout guard:** run every `codex exec` / `codex exec resume` with a 10-minute ceiling so any future stall fails loud instead of hanging silently. Via Claude Code's Bash tool, pass `timeout: 600000` on the tool call (the default 2-minute tool timeout is too short for real reviews and would kill them mid-run). In a plain shell, prefix the command with `timeout 600` (Linux / Git Bash) or `gtimeout 600` (macOS via coreutils — stock macOS has no `timeout`). If the ceiling trips, treat it as a failed run: stop and tell the user rather than retrying blind. Applies to the resume call below too.

**Rounds 2..MAX** (resume the SAME session — Codex remembers its earlier critiques, won't re-litigate settled points):

```bash
# NOTE: resume rejects -s. Force read-only via -c sandbox_mode, or Codex
# inherits config.toml (possibly danger-full-access) and could write files.
codex exec resume "$THREAD_ID" -c sandbox_mode="read-only" --json \
  -o /tmp/codex-verdict.txt \
  "I revised the plan. Re-review PLAN.md. Same rules. End with VERDICT: APPROVED or VERDICT: REVISE." \
  < /dev/null 2>/dev/null >/dev/null
```

Both `codex exec` and `codex exec resume` support `--json` (stream → parse `thread_id` first round) and `-o/--output-last-message` (verdict capture).

**Each round, after Codex returns:**
1. Read `/tmp/codex-verdict.txt`. Append to `LOG_FILE`: `## Round <n> — Codex` + the full critique.
2. Grep the last line for the verdict token.
   - `VERDICT: APPROVED` → break the loop, go to Step 3 (converged).
   - `VERDICT: REVISE` → Claude reads the critique, decides **what's actually worth acting on** (Claude has final say — Codex advises, it does not command). Revise `PLAN_FILE`. Append to `LOG_FILE`: `### Claude's response` + what you changed and what you rejected and why. Increment `ROUND`.
3. If `ROUND > MAX_ROUNDS` → break to Step 3 (deadlock).
4. **Triage before you spend a round.** Sort every finding into one of three buckets first:
   - *Technical* — Claude decides and revises. Normal loop.
   - *Product / scope / pedagogy* — a decision only Joshua can make (what the feature should do, what tradeoff is acceptable, what is in scope). **Do not spend a round arguing it.** Stop the loop, put it to Joshua with `AskUserQuestion`, and fold his answer into `PLAN_FILE` before resuming. Rounds have been burned re-litigating decisions Codex was never able to settle.
   - *Already rejected* — see the re-assertion rule below.
5. **Re-assertion guard.** When a round returns a finding Claude rejected in an earlier round, do not re-argue it. Check whether the critique cites **new evidence** (a file, a line, a failure mode not previously raised). If not, log it as `re-assertion, no new evidence — held` and move on without revising. Carry the standing rejections into the resume prompt so Codex knows what has already been settled: append `Already settled (do not re-raise without new evidence): <list>` to each resume message.

### Step 3 — Resolution (human gate #2)

**If APPROVED:** Present to the user — the final `PLAN_FILE`, a 3-bullet summary of what the argument improved, and the round count. Ask: *"Plan survived N rounds of Codex. Implement it now — Codex builds it (`/codex-build`), Claude builds it, or stop here?"* Only on a yes is code written. **No code is written during the loop.** If the user picks Codex, invoke the `codex-build` skill with `SPEC_FILE=PLAN.md` and the same `LOG_FILE` — roles flip (Codex writes, Claude reviews the diff) and the build rounds append to the same log.

**If MAX_ROUNDS hit without APPROVED (deadlock):** Do NOT pretend it converged. Surface the unresolved disagreements explicitly: list each point Codex still flags and Claude's counter-position. Hand it to the human to break the tie. This is a legitimate, useful outcome — a flagged disagreement beats a false "approved."

## Hard rules

- Codex is read-only EVERY round — `-s read-only` for the first call, `-c sandbox_mode="read-only"` for every resume (resume has no `-s`). It never writes. If you're tempted to give it write access, stop — that's a different skill.
- The loop ALWAYS terminates at `MAX_ROUNDS`. No unbounded recursion.
- Claude is the final arbiter on every REVISE — incorporate good critiques, reject bad ones *with a reason logged*. Don't cave to Codex on everything (that defeats the cross-model check) and don't ignore it (that defeats the point).
- **Never spend a round on a decision only Joshua can make.** Product, scope, and pedagogy questions go to him immediately via `AskUserQuestion`, not into the argument. Codex cannot resolve them and each attempt costs a full repo re-read.
- **Rounds resume the SAME thread.** Every round after the first is `codex exec resume "$THREAD_ID"`. If `thread_id` capture failed, fix the capture — do not fall back to a fresh `codex exec`, which re-reads the whole repo and loses everything already settled. Observed rounds of 3 to 5 have mostly been this failure, not genuine disagreement.
- Code only after human gate #2.
- `LOG_FILE` is the deliverable — it tells the whole story of the argument. Keep it complete.

## What NOT to do

- Don't use this to review existing code — that's `/codex:review`.
- Don't pin a `-codex` model variant on ChatGPT-account auth — it 400s.
- Don't skip the log — the argument transcript is the most valuable artifact.
- Don't let Codex edit files. Read-only, always.


ARGUMENTS: Amend the Classroom Home v3 plan (brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN.md) with Joshua's Rounds 11-12 decisions from brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-visual-upgrade.md: booking de-emphasised (slim next-session line, Book pill with credits, quiet pay-per-session link, no renewal pitch), no default RRC tile, analytics back (accuracy trend + week volume, improved), and settle the open layout points (freed column / main card width, replacement tile). Re-check against the six reference images. Codex model: gpt-6-astra (reviewer role). PR 1 build is already in the lsatu-home-v2 worktree uncommitted.

# Plan: Classroom Home v3, first build
_Act 5, Round 3 revision by Claude, 2026-09-23. Supersedes the phase plan (history in the vault's git and in PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md). Codex's Act 5 round 1 reviewed the superseded file by mistake; its findings that apply to v3 are folded in here._

Sources: decisions `brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-visual-upgrade.md` (rounds 1-10, converged Q2-Q7); research `research/2026-09-23-classroom-home-dashboard-research/`; design v3 `Home3.dc.html` on https://claude.ai/artifact/6zF3FgewiMA7pCZSk766Xt (local: scratchpad `artifact-files/307bbd6c-86e0-43cd-a5df-8735d306b8fb/project/Home3.dc.html`).

## Situation
- App `projects/lsatu`: React + Vite + TS + Tailwind, TanStack Query, Supabase via Lovable Cloud. Home = `/classroom` in the Classroom frame (glossary `src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md`).
- Home code: `src/components/classroom/home/`.
- PR #133 (branch `feat/home-v2-phase1`, unmerged): `pickContinueWork`, `QuestionFinder` `open()` handle, container-query columns, preservation tests. v3 builds on it and closes #133. #133 has NO Section tab; "Timed section" only opens the finder.
- Hard rules: completed student work never lost or modified; additive migrations only; branch + PR; Lovable Cloud Edge Functions deploy through Lovable (confirm first); frontend reaches lsatprep.study only via Lovable deploy; a teacher is never the student; student copy per `wiki/teaching-voice.md`, no em dashes.
- Builder: Codex `gpt-6-sol`, low. Spec/review: Claude + `gpt-6-astra`.

## Goal
Ship the v3 Classroom Home for students: a warm top panel (greeting, two numbers, Question finder, Continue, Smart Drill, Timed section, Inbox), a dominant visual insight that tells the student what to do next, a precise per-type list, supporting tiles, and the unchanged Next seven days strip. Every number says what it counts; the page is fast, stable, accessible, and complete with zero coach input.

## Approach

### 7. Sure but wrong (gated)
Claude runs a read-only audit before PR 4: share of last-30-day `attempts` with non-null `confidence`, per write path, and whether each path captures confidence before feedback. Ship only if ≥60% coverage for at least half of active students; else defer. Card: labelled 2x2 (1-2 unsure, 4-5 sure); neutral 3 and missing ratings are excluded and disclosed in the coverage line ("[n] rated sure or unsure, [m] neutral, [k] unrated"); floor = 12 first attempts that land in a cell; tap a cell for its questions.

### 8. Quality bar (release requirement)
Cache-first paint on return visits; layout-matched skeletons on first load; no spinners. Count-up once per local day; motion transform/opacity only; none under `prefers-reduced-motion`. WCAG 2.2 AA incl. 200% text, focus order, contrast on gradient and taupe. Accommodated timer used for any pace figure. Verification: a student test account at 390 / 900 / 1440 and docked widths, dark class, keyboard only, reduced motion. Measure Home first paint before and after PR 2.

### 9. Build order (PRs)
1. Layout + top panel + tiles + Book-a-session state on existing data, on top of #133 (closes #133).
2. RPCs (with auth tests) + adapters + insight engine + honest visuals + list + evidence drawer + `excludeAnswered`.
3. Registry (after Joshua's review) + diagnosis-to-action + Ask draft.
4. Sure but wrong, only if the audit passes.
Build two (separate plan): session brief; review then fresh check; Today shortcut; coach pinning with optional note; unified notifications (event identity, read semantics, reminders); RC lens; launcher Section tab reusing `rcSectionHref` (`rcSections.ts:131`) and `kind: 'section'`.

## Key decisions & tradeoffs
- Diagnosis leads; coach notes never required.
- Bubbles for character, list for precision and accessibility.
- Frozen insight per visit.
- Small human-reviewed registry, not inference.
- Sure but wrong gated by audit, no new rating step.
- Unified notifications deferred whole, so its identity and read-state design is done once, properly.

## Risks / open questions
- RPC cost on first paint.
- Registry quality depends on one review.
- Student test account needed.
- Taupe/gradient contrast may force darker text than the mockup.

## Out of scope
Slim menu rail; notifications merge and bell popover; pinning; session brief; review loop; Today; RC lens; launcher tabs; goal score, streaks, PT score, month calendar; AI text; teacher view redesign; public landing page.

## user

Tool result:
Amendment A needs revision. The product decisions are clear; the data contracts and final composition are not.

1. **A4: choose full width, with internal columns.** Keeping an unexplained second tile beside the main insight would recreate the filler-card problem. But stretching PR 1’s text-heavy assignment card across the page will not deliver the references’ visual hierarchy.  
   **Fix:** Use a full-width main card, with diagnosis/action beside bubbles in PR 2, bounded text width, and a compact content-driven height in PR 1; stack internally at narrow container widths.

2. **A5: Last section belongs in PR 2.** It requires the honest sections adapter, not merely another read. The existing [sectionHistory.ts](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/lib/sectionHistory.ts:107) reads all rows, substitutes zero scores, and can fabricate a completion date from the current time.  
   **Fix:** Ship Last section with a bounded adapter in PR 2; select the latest known completion date, preserve unknown scores/denominators, and verify that Review opens that saved result rather than starting a fresh section.

3. **A3: LR versus RC is resolved by storage, not question-type guessing.** The inspected LR writers use `attempts`; RC uses its separate progress API. The existing `drill_history` SQL explicitly labels `attempts` as `lr` and unions RC separately. However, introductory LR work also has separate storage, so this is not automatically “all LR practice.”  
   **Fix:** Source this release’s charts from `attempts`, document the included practice paths, and disclose excluded introductory/other activity instead of inferring LR from `qtype` or section number.

4. **“First attempts only” is not implemented by excluding review modes.** [Drill.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/pages/Drill.tsx:3476) writes blind-review rows using the original session mode. Selecting the earliest row *inside eight weeks* would also misclassify an older question’s retry as first exposure.  
   **Fix:** Exclude explicit BR/replay records, select the earliest eligible attempt per student/question across history, then apply the chart window; test older originals, retries and BR rows sharing a mode.

5. **Undated rows create uncertainty beyond missing chart dates.** Dropping an undated original can make a later dated retry look like a first attempt. An “undated” count alone does not solve that.  
   **Fix:** Exclude questions with ambiguous undated non-review history from first-attempt accuracy, report that exclusion separately, and never invent timestamps or rewrite historical rows.

6. **The volume tile has conflicting definitions.** “This week,” “last seven days,” distinct questions and questions answered are different measures. Section 1 also retains a competing `home_week_counts` RPC.  
   **Fix:** Choose “LR questions · last 7 days,” count distinct eligible question IDs per local day with repeats across days allowed and disclosed, sum those bars for the headline, and replace the obsolete count RPC with `home_lr_activity`.

7. **The trend can imply evidence that does not exist.** “Three qualifying weeks” is undefined; smoothing can overshoot or bridge empty weeks; the current week is incomplete and may contain no attempts.  
   **Fix:** Require three weeks with at least ten eligible questions, retain missing weeks as gaps, use non-overshooting interpolation, label the current week “so far,” and glow only an actual point.

8. **The adjacent accuracy displays use different populations.** A3 uses first attempts, while section 2’s per-type list uses the latest attempt within each bucket. Both could show different accuracy for the same work without explanation.  
   **Fix:** Explicitly label “First-attempt accuracy” versus “Latest attempt per question,” expose numerator/denominator and date window, and avoid presenting their differences as comparable improvement.

9. **A1 assumes a booking link the Home model does not carry.** `nextBooking` has an ID/source/meeting URL, but the current session mapping retains none of those. Separately, `resolveStudentBookingCta` returns renewals and a `/booking` fallback; its result has no explicit branch identifier.  
   **Fix:** Add a typed Home-specific booking state with numeric credits and a verified booking destination; select single-session offers explicitly, preserve schedule access gates, and distinguish loading/error from confirmed no booking.

10. **PR 2’s main-card replacement risks losing assigned work.** A4 changes the taupe card from assignment hero/list to diagnosis/bubbles without specifying where the existing rows and their actions go. Continue is not a substitute for the full assignment list.  
    **Fix:** Name a permanent assigned-work location and preserve completion, briefing and report-card actions; test that assigned RRC survives removal of its default tile.

11. **The proposed chart row only partly delivers the references’ character.** Two equal dark rectangles add charts, but the references also rely on unequal visual weight, large readable numbers, substantial plotting space and compact supporting information. A tall greeting plus an unbounded assignment card could bury both charts.  
    **Fix:** Specify a wider accuracy tile and narrower volume tile, coordinated heights, prominent real values, direct labels and generous plot areas; verify their placement in populated student views at narrow, mid, wide and docked widths.

12. **The amended layout still has contradictory instructions.** Amendment A only overrides sections 1 and 9, yet also changes section 5’s bars; the old mobile order still includes Next session, and the per-type list’s final location is unclear. Optional-message removal can also leave a half-empty lower grid.  
    **Fix:** Publish one authoritative desktop/mobile order covering assignments, insight, charts, list, Last section and optional message, with explicit collapse rules for every absent tile.

13. **A6 repeats the previous delivery failure unless the release boundary changes.** PR 1 still has no charts, although their absence triggered this amendment. Separate implementation PRs are reasonable; calling PR 1 the delivered visual upgrade is not.  
    **Fix:** Keep PR 1 as an intermediate build and make acceptance of the visual upgrade depend on PR 2’s real-data charts and browser evidence, including sparse, error and no-coach-message states.

Useful reference elements still missing:

- **Be.run’s progress with a clear endpoint:** use verified completed/total counts for assigned work or bootcamps; do not make segmented accuracy bars resemble task completion.
- **Homies’ metric hierarchy:** give existing accuracy and volume values typographic prominence rather than adding more metrics.
- **Notton/Mountain/WeatherWise’s depth:** use restrained surface gradients and chart-area lighting behind actual data, with static reduced-motion treatment; decorative landscape data would add no student value.
- **Coders’ compact work overview:** make assignments easy to scan by title, due date and real progress, preserving the existing menu and unchanged week strip.

One scope issue: the working tree also contains **589 deleted lines in `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`**, outside this Home amendment. **Fix:** Exclude that unrelated diff from Home staging and review it separately.

This was a read-only plan and source review; I did not run the app or independently verify the recorded test results.

## user

Tool result:
Amendment A is substantially clearer, but three implementation details still need revision.

1. **BR answers are separate rows, but “`br_*` set” is an unsafe exclusion rule.** The original answer is inserted at [Drill.tsx:1609](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/pages/Drill.tsx:1609). BR completion inserts additional rows with `br_selected: true` at [Drill.tsx:3479](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/pages/Drill.tsx:3479); it does not update the original row, despite the misleading “Update attempts” comment. However, a migration gives `br_marked`, `br_selected` and `br_changed` defaults of `false`. Testing whether *any BR field is non-null* could therefore exclude ordinary answers.  
   **Fix:** Define the predicate explicitly: exclude `br_selected IS TRUE` or populated BR-result fields such as `br_answer`/`br_outcome`; preserve false/null flags and test an original answer followed by BR.

2. **BR metadata does not identify every repeat or “replay.”** A3 conflates BR rows, repeated practice and session recordings. Recorded replays live in `session_replays`; ordinary later attempts need not contain BR fields. Accuracy correctly handles those through first-attempt selection, while volume intentionally counts practice repeated on another day.  
   **Fix:** Separate the rules: exclude BR-result rows from both metrics, deduplicate across history for accuracy and within each local day for volume, and remove the undefined “replay records” predicate.

3. **A7 still risks burying the charts, especially on phones.** The current top panel contains the greeting, finder, Continue and actions. The next full-width insight then stacks diagnosis/action above bubbles below a 900px container width. Consequently, both charts follow two substantial stacked sections. Their 60% plot-area requirement controls their own interiors, not how far down the page they appear.  
   **Fix:** Put the chart row immediately after the top panel in PR 2, followed by the full-width insight; on phones use top panel—including one Continue—then accuracy, volume and insight, with browser checks at specified viewport heights as well as widths.

**`/booking` is a valid booked-session destination.** [Booking.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/pages/Booking.tsx:20) opens the Schedule shell’s Book tab. Its `CalendlyBookingCard` renders “Your upcoming sessions,” including the no-pass branch and expired/exhausted-pass states. Schedule’s existing Manage booking action also uses that tab. Keep this destination. It opens the booking-management surface, rather than selecting an individual booking; the amendment should not promise an individual-session deep link.

Two smaller contracts remain worth tightening:

4. **Sparse weeks silently disappear.** The revision now creates points only for weeks with at least ten questions. It specifies gaps for empty weeks but does not distinguish an empty week from one containing nine valid answers. With three qualifying weeks elsewhere, a student’s current practice could become invisible.  
   **Fix:** Keep all eight weekly buckets in the accessible data, show sub-threshold weeks as labelled insufficient-sample gaps or hollow unconnected markers, and reserve zero/no-practice wording for actual zero counts.

5. **The source disclosure overgeneralizes “bootcamp” exclusion.** [LRQuestionModal.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-home-v2/src/components/drill/shared/LRQuestionModal.tsx:174) writes into `attempts`, with default mode `bootcamp-pf`. Thus some bootcamp work is included, while separately stored introductory exercises are excluded.  
   **Fix:** Describe coverage by storage/write path: include bank-question attempts, including those launched inside bootcamps; exclude separately stored introductory exercises and RC.

The full-width main card and Last section in PR 2 remain settled. The revised chart proportions, real-number hierarchy, restrained lighting and genuine assignment progress adequately translate the references without adding prohibited metrics. The remaining visual problem is placement, not missing decorative features.

Read-only review; no files changed and no runtime verification performed.

VERDICT: REVISE

## user

Tool result:
Amendment A is sound enough to implement. The revised BR predicate matches the inspected writer: BR answers are separate rows, while ordinary rows with false/null BR flags remain eligible. Accuracy and volume now have distinct deduplication rules, and the coverage description correctly includes bank-question attempts launched inside bootcamps.

I accept insight-first ordering. Moving the mobile bubbles below the charts preserves diagnosis-to-action while reducing the content above analytics. The viewport-height checks make chart visibility testable; I don’t think chart-first ordering needs escalation to Joshua.

Three small specification corrections remain, none requiring another design round:

- **Zero first attempts does not mean “no practice.”** A student could spend that week answering previously attempted questions. **Fix:** Label zero accuracy buckets “No first attempts,” leaving volume to describe practice activity.
- **“Three point-weeks” is ambiguous now that hollow markers exist.** **Fix:** Say “three weeks with at least ten eligible first attempts”; hollow markers do not count toward the line-chart threshold.
- **The phone order omits the relocated bubbles and lists Continue separately despite placing it inside the top panel.** **Fix:** Write the order explicitly: top panel containing one Continue → diagnosis/action → accuracy → volume → bubbles → per-type list → remaining tiles.

Treat the height limits as layout acceptance checks at normal text size, not fixed-height containers that clip content at 200% text enlargement. Approval is for the plan; runtime, accessibility and real-data verification remain required.

VERDICT: APPROVED

## assistant

Codex and I agreed on the revised plan after 3 rounds, and nothing is left for you to settle. Codex found 16 problems in total and I fixed all of them. Nothing has been built from this plan yet; it's ready to go.

**What "the main card" is:** it's the big beige card under the greeting. In PR 1 it holds "Pick up where you left off" and your students' assigned work. In PR 2 it becomes the diagnosis card, which says what to work on next and shows the bubbles. It will now run the full width of the page.

**What's settled:**
- **Booking gets much quieter.** The big dark Next session card is gone.
  - A booked session is one line under the greeting: "Next session: Thursday 7:15 pm".
  - A student with credits and nothing booked gets a small "Book · 3 left" button beside Smart Drill.
  - A student with no credits gets a same-size "Book a session" button with the pay-per-session link, never a package or renewal pitch.
- **Read, Recall, Colour** only appears on Home when you assign it.
- **The analytics come back in PR 2,** as a row of two chart tiles right under the diagnosis card:
  - **Accuracy over 8 weeks:** a line of first-attempt accuracy, with a glowing point on this week, like the WeatherWise curve. Weeks with only a few questions show as hollow marks, so a thin week never looks like a real trend.
  - **LR questions in the last 7 days:** daily bars with the total as a big number, like the Notton bars and the Homies numbers.
- **Assigned work gets its own card** in PR 2. A **Last section** tile goes next to it, showing the latest timed section's score and date and linking to its review.
- Codex wanted the charts above the diagnosis card. I kept the diagnosis first because you chose that it leads. Codex accepted that once I made sure the charts can't get pushed far down the page on a phone.

**Codex caught three things that would have shown wrong numbers:**
- Blind-review answers are saved as extra rows, so they would have been counted twice.
- Questions with no date could have made a retry look like a first try.
- Some bootcamp questions are counted and some aren't, so the charts now say exactly what they include.

**Where it stands:** PR 1 is not the finished upgrade. The redesign isn't done until PR 2's charts are in and checked in the browser. You're signed in on the dev copy, but I haven't looked at `/classroom` yet. I'll screenshot it after the next build.

Records: [the plan, Amendment A](brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN.md), [the Codex debate](brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md), and your answers in [the brainstorm file](brainstorms/2026-09-23-classroom-home-visual-upgrade.md).

## user

Tool result:
CONTEXT-MAP.md
**Classroom frame** — the persistent layout every Classroom route is drawn in: the cream left rail, an optional middle list, and the white reading surface. Implemented by `ClassroomLayout`. Navigating between surfaces it draws swaps the content only and never remounts the rail. A page "sits in the Classroom frame" regardless of its URL; `/flagged` and `/journal` both do.
**StudentShell** — the older, separate frame with its own 240px rail, still drawing `/mainhub`, `/practice` and the rest. A student moving between a Classroom-frame page and a StudentShell page sees the whole page rebuild. Reducing that boundary is ongoing work, one surface at a time.
**Reading column** — the white content column. Most routes use one reading column on the right. A Wrong answers detail uses a four-column Classroom frame: cream rail, entries list, vertical question, and review panel. Below its journal-only breakpoints the entries list becomes an overlay and the review panel moves under the question.
**Drill landing workspace** — the pre-question surface at `/classroom/drill`, drawn inside the Classroom frame. It includes drill selection, launch controls, practice insights, and today's work; it excludes the official question-answering experience.
**Question finder** — the one control that turns something a student can say about a question into that question opened. It takes free text, matched against question text, and it takes a question address (PT 90, Section 1, Question 12) typed or picked from PrepTest / Section / Question selectors. It searches both banks and groups its results by bank, Logical Reasoning first, because a hit lands differently in each: an LR hit opens that one question, an RC hit opens its whole passage with every question on it. The finder is a finding aid, not the scope of the work it opens. It appears on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing workspace; it never builds a set, it hands that to the Drill console.
**Drill console** — the control surface inside the Drill landing workspace where a student assembles a set: the filters, the preview of the exact set, and the start action. Finding one named question is not its job; that is the Question finder.
**Drill canvas** — the route-specific visual composition to the right of the persistent Classroom rail, containing the Drill hero, Drill console, practice/insight workspace, and Today focus. The Classroom rail is not part of the canvas.
**Section** — a middle-column list paired with the reading column: Notes and recaps, Assigned work, Flagged. A section is picked in the rail, lists its items in the middle column, and shows the chosen item in the reading column.
**Flag** — a come-back-to-this marker the student raises *during* a question, from a drill or a blind review. It records intent to return, and says nothing about whether the question was answered correctly. Stored in `flagged_questions`.
**Flagged question** — a question carrying a flag. The Flagged section is the student's unfinished business: questions they chose to set aside. Clearing a flag is the student's own act (Unflag), not something the system does when they get the question right.
**Wrong answer** — a question the student actually missed, recorded in `wrong_answer_journal` and worked through in Wrong answers at `/journal`. Distinct from a flag in both meaning and storage: a flag is chosen, a wrong answer is earned. A question can be both, one, or neither. Do not treat the two lists as views of the same thing.
**Journal entry** — the review of one wrong answer at `/journal/:qid`. The URL is the selection: it resolves only by `qid`, never by the journal row id, and `/journal` does not silently select the first entry. Its review fields autosave without a visible save state.
**Ask** — sending a question to the coach from the surface the student is looking at, with their own comment attached. Always labelled "Ask", never with the coach's name; the name belongs in messaging itself, not in the furniture that leads to it.

## assistant

I checked the tester view on desktop and phone. The changes work, and I fixed four visual problems I found along the way. Typecheck is clean and 119 of 119 tests pass. Nothing is committed.

**What works:**
- **Days to test:** "196 days to test day" shows under the greeting.
- **Continue card:** the dark card shows the next assignment, PT156.S4 (LR), with the orange arrow.
- **Book button:** it shows "Book · 5 left", and clicking it opens `/booking`.
- **No booking card:** the big dark booking card is gone. The tester has no session booked, so the "Next session" line doesn't show, which is correct.
- **Inbox:** it shows 1 unread, and "From Joshua Fisseha" shows the latest message.
- **Read, Recall, Colour:** there's no default tile. It appears only as the tester's assigned row.
- **Main card:** it is full width and holds "Start here" plus the two assigned rows.
- **Next seven days:** unchanged.
- **Sideways scroll:** none on desktop or phone.

**What I fixed:**
1. **Empty bar under "Open the set":** the hero's stacked-paper decoration showed as an empty cream bar. I hid it on the beige card.
2. **Stray box:** the hero's orange glow showed as a box on the right side of the beige card. I hid that too.
3. **Uneven assigned rows:** the two rows ended at different widths and had square corners. They now line up, with rounded corners and space between them.
4. **Phone buttons:** Book sat alone at half width. The buttons now sit two per row, and Book has a calendar icon like the other buttons.

**Two things I think need your call:**
- **PT156.S4 appears three times:** in the Continue card, in "Start here", and as a row in Assigned to you. I'd keep the Continue card and the row, and have "Start here" show something other than what Continue already shows.
- **The Read, Recall, Colour row is cramped on a phone:** the title gets cut to "Read, Re..." because the Open and Mark done buttons take the space. That's how the rows already behaved before this change. It could be fixed in this PR or later.

Should I fix the triple listing now, or commit PR 1 as it is on a new branch and open the pull request?

}) {
  const { hero } = model;
  const finderRef = React.useRef<QuestionFinderHandle>(null);
  const continueRow = pickContinueWork(
    model.continueRow !== undefined ? (model.continueRow ? [model.continueRow] : []) : model.rows,
    model.access,
    model.isAdmin,
  );
  const [briefing, setBriefing] = React.useState<SectionBriefingData | null>(null);

  /**
   * A timed section is the one kind of assigned work whose page was only ever
   * instructions. Opening it raises those instructions over Home instead of
   * navigating: the student is one Escape from where they were, and Enter is
   * the only thing that starts a clock.
   *
   * Keyed by route so the hero, the work rows, and anything else that opens by
   * route all behave the same without a second callback threaded through.
   */
  const briefingByRoute = React.useMemo(() => {
    const map = new Map<string, SectionBriefingData>();
    const add = (row?: HomeRow | null) => {
      if (row?.briefing) map.set(row.route, row.briefing);
    };
    add(model.hero?.row);
    add(continueRow);
    model.rows.forEach(add);
    return map;
  }, [model.hero, model.rows, continueRow]);

  const handleOpen = React.useCallback(
    (route: string) => {
      const found = briefingByRoute.get(route);
      if (found) setBriefing(found);
      else onOpen(route);
    },
    [briefingByRoute, onOpen],
  );

  /*
   * Opening a Report Card is what closes it.
    enabled: !!heroActivity && !!user,
    staleTime: 15_000,
    queryFn: async () => listItemResponses(heroActivity!.id, user!.id),
  });

  const hero = React.useMemo(() => {
    if (!heroRow) return null;
    const started = !heroRow.isNew;
    const total = heroActivity?.item_count ?? 0;
    const answered = responses
      ? new Set(responses.map((response) => response.item_order)).size
      : null;
    return {
      row: heroRow,
      eyebrow: started ? "PICK UP WHERE YOU LEFT OFF" : "START HERE",
      kindLabel: KIND_LABEL[heroRow.kind].toUpperCase(),
      meta: stripKind(heroRow.meta, heroRow.kind),
      prose: heroActivity?.instructions?.tagline ?? null,
      primaryLabel: primaryLabelFor(heroRow.kind, started && (answered ?? 0) > 0),
      progress:
        heroActivity && total > 0
          ? {
              completed: answered,
              total,
              unit: total === 1 ? "prompt" : "prompts",
              estimateMinutes: null,
            }
          : null,
    };
  }, [heroActivity, heroRow, responses]);

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
